| [ ] | `crawl.customSearch.description` | Define selectors before crawling. Values are extracted from the fetched HTML and stored in that crawl run; older snapshots are not fetched again. Each query stores up to 5 values of 1,000 characters per URL, with a shared 64,000-character limit per run. Selected values are persisted in project data. CSS and bounded XPath queries use the parsed DOM; Regex matches the decoded HTML source and returns the first capture group when present. XPath supports / and // paths, attribute predicates, contains(@attr, …), starts-with(@attr, …), not(@attr), @attr!=…, contains(text(), …), contains(., …), text()='…', positions, and terminal /text() or /@attr; unsupported syntax is reported as an error. | Define selectors before crawling. Values are extracted from the fetched HTML and stored in that crawl run; older snapshots are not fetched again. Each query stores up to 5 values of 1,000 characters per URL, with a shared 64,000-character limit per run. Selected values are persisted in project data. CSS and bounded XPath queries use the parsed DOM; Regex matches the decoded HTML source and returns the first capture group when present. XPath supports / and // paths, attribute predicates, contains(@attr, …), starts-with(@attr, …), not(@attr), @attr!=…, contains(text(), …), contains(., …), text()='…', positions, and terminal /text() or /@attr; unsupported syntax is reported as an error. | same-as-en |  |

| [ ] | `help.overview.healthScore.title` | Health score | Health score | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.healthScore.what` | A single number from 0 to 100 that sums up how many technical and on-page SEO problems SEOmi found on this one page. 100 means nothing wrong was detected; every problem subtracts points, and serious problems subtract more than small ones. | A single number from 0 to 100 that sums up how many technical and on-page SEO problems SEOmi found on this one page. 100 means nothing wrong was detected; every problem subtracts points, and serious problems subtract more than small ones. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.healthScore.why` | Search engines and visitors both prefer pages that load, can be read and are easy to understand. A low score usually means something is blocking the page from ranking, such as a missing title, a broken status code or a noindex tag. The score is a quick triage tool, not a ranking factor that Google reads. | Search engines and visitors both prefer pages that load, can be read and are easy to understand. A low score usually means something is blocking the page from ranking, such as a missing title, a broken status code or a noindex tag. The score is a quick triage tool, not a ranking factor that Google reads. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.healthScore.action` | Check the colour: 85 and above (green) is healthy, 65 to 84 (amber) needs attention, below 65 (red) needs work now. Open the issues list further down this screen and start with the Critical items. Fix one issue, run the audit again and watch the score rise. Do not chase 100 at any price: a score of 90 with great content beats 100 on a thin page. | Check the colour: 85 and above (green) is healthy, 65 to 84 (amber) needs attention, below 65 (red) needs work now. Open the issues list further down this screen and start with the Critical items. Fix one issue, run the audit again and watch the score rise. Do not chase 100 at any price: a score of 90 with great content beats 100 on a thin page. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.healthScore.example` | A page with a missing title (Critical), no meta description (Warning) and two images without alt text (Info) might score 68. Adding the title and the description usually brings it above 85. | A page with a missing title (Critical), no meta description (Warning) and two images without alt text (Info) might score 68. Adding the title and the description usually brings it above 85. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.healthScore.learnMore` | The score only covers what SEOmi can measure on this single page. It does not judge your content quality, backlinks or competition. | The score only covers what SEOmi can measure on this single page. It does not judge your content quality, backlinks or competition. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.httpStatus.title` | HTTP status code | HTTP status code | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.httpStatus.what` | A three-digit answer the web server gives to every request, saying whether the page was delivered. 200 means OK, 301 and 302 mean the page moved, 404 means not found, 410 means gone for good, and 500 or higher means the server itself failed. | A three-digit answer the web server gives to every request, saying whether the page was delivered. 200 means OK, 301 and 302 mean the page moved, 404 means not found, 410 means gone for good, and 500 or higher means the server itself failed. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.httpStatus.why` | Google only indexes pages that answer 200 (or follow a redirect to one). A 404 or 5xx page drops out of search results, and visitors see an error. Even a short-lived 500 can make Googlebot visit your site less often. | Google only indexes pages that answer 200 (or follow a redirect to one). A 404 or 5xx page drops out of search results, and visitors see an error. Even a short-lived 500 can make Googlebot visit your site less often. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.httpStatus.action` | 200: nothing to do, this is correct. 301 or 302: check the final address is the page you want people to land on. Use 301 for permanent moves. 404 or 410: restore the page, or redirect the old address to the closest matching page with a 301. 5xx: ask your hosting provider or developer to check the server logs, then re-run the audit. After a fix, re-test to confirm the status is now 200. | 200: nothing to do, this is correct. 301 or 302: check the final address is the page you want people to land on. Use 301 for permanent moves. 404 or 410: restore the page, or redirect the old address to the closest matching page with a 301. 5xx: ask your hosting provider or developer to check the server logs, then re-run the audit. After a fix, re-test to confirm the status is now 200. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.httpStatus.example` | You renamed /blog/seo-tips to /blog/seo-guide and forgot the redirect. The old address now returns 404, so every link and Google result pointing to it leads to an error. A 301 from the old to the new address fixes it. | You renamed /blog/seo-tips to /blog/seo-guide and forgot the redirect. The old address now returns 404, so every link and Google result pointing to it leads to an error. A 301 from the old to the new address fixes it. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.responseTime.title` | Response time | Response time | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.responseTime.what` | How many milliseconds (1000 ms = 1 second) the server needed to start sending this page after SEOmi asked for it. It measures the server, not how long the page takes to display in a browser. | How many milliseconds (1000 ms = 1 second) the server needed to start sending this page after SEOmi asked for it. It measures the server, not how long the page takes to display in a browser. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.responseTime.why` | A slow server delays everything that comes after it, so visitors leave and Google crawls fewer of your pages. Google suggests keeping server response under about 200 ms. Above 1000 ms is clearly slow. | A slow server delays everything that comes after it, so visitors leave and Google crawls fewer of your pages. Google suggests keeping server response under about 200 ms. Above 1000 ms is clearly slow. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.responseTime.action` | Under 200 ms: excellent, leave it. 200 to 600 ms: acceptable; consider caching if the page is mostly static. Above 600 ms: turn on page caching (or a CDN) and compress responses. Above 1000 ms: check hosting quality, heavy database queries and slow plugins. Measure two or three times, because a single reading can be a one-off spike. | Under 200 ms: excellent, leave it. 200 to 600 ms: acceptable; consider caching if the page is mostly static. Above 600 ms: turn on page caching (or a CDN) and compress responses. Above 1000 ms: check hosting quality, heavy database queries and slow plugins. Measure two or three times, because a single reading can be a one-off spike. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.responseTime.example` | A blog post that answers in 1800 ms on shared hosting often drops to 150 to 300 ms after a full-page cache plugin is switched on. | A blog post that answers in 1800 ms on shared hosting often drops to 150 to 300 ms after a full-page cache plugin is switched on. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.severityCounts.title` | Issue severity counts | Issue severity counts | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.severityCounts.what` | The problems found on this page, grouped by how serious they are. Critical issues can stop the page from ranking or working. Warnings are real weaknesses that cost you traffic or clicks. Info items are small improvements or things worth knowing. | The problems found on this page, grouped by how serious they are. Critical issues can stop the page from ranking or working. Warnings are real weaknesses that cost you traffic or clicks. Info items are small improvements or things worth knowing. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.severityCounts.why` | Grouping tells you what to fix first. Ten Info items matter less than one Critical, so you avoid spending a day on cosmetic details while a broken canonical tag hides the page from Google. | Grouping tells you what to fix first. Ten Info items matter less than one Critical, so you avoid spending a day on cosmetic details while a broken canonical tag hides the page from Google. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.severityCounts.action` | Fix every Critical issue first, then re-run the audit. Work through the Warnings, starting with the ones that affect many pages. Treat Info items as a backlog for when you have time. A 0 under Critical is the goal, not 0 everywhere. | Fix every Critical issue first, then re-run the audit. Work through the Warnings, starting with the ones that affect many pages. Treat Info items as a backlog for when you have time. A 0 under Critical is the goal, not 0 everywhere. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.severityCounts.example` | 3 Critical, 5 Warning, 9 Info: start with the 3 Critical ones, for example a missing title, a noindex tag and a 404 on the main image. | 3 Critical, 5 Warning, 9 Info: start with the 3 Critical ones, for example a missing title, a noindex tag and a 404 on the main image. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.wordCount.title` | Word count | Word count | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.wordCount.what` | The number of words of visible text that SEOmi found in the main content of the page. Menus, scripts and hidden code are not counted. The reading time is estimated at about 200 words per minute. | The number of words of visible text that SEOmi found in the main content of the page. Menus, scripts and hidden code are not counted. The reading time is estimated at about 200 words per minute. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.wordCount.why` | Pages with very little text give search engines little to understand, which is called thin content. Long is not automatically better, though: the page must fully answer what the visitor came for. | Pages with very little text give search engines little to understand, which is called thin content. Long is not automatically better, though: the page must fully answer what the visitor came for. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.wordCount.action` | Under 300 words: ask yourself whether the page really answers the question. Add useful detail if not. 300 to 1000 words: normal for most service and product pages. Over 1500 words: typical for in-depth guides; make sure headings split it into scannable sections. Compare with the top Google results for your keyword, not with a magic number. | Under 300 words: ask yourself whether the page really answers the question. Add useful detail if not. 300 to 1000 words: normal for most service and product pages. Over 1500 words: typical for in-depth guides; make sure headings split it into scannable sections. Compare with the top Google results for your keyword, not with a magic number. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.overview.wordCount.example` | A product page with 80 words usually loses to competitors who explain materials, sizes, shipping and returns in 400 words. | A product page with 80 words usually loses to competitors who explain materials, sizes, shipping and returns in 400 words. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.titleLength.title` | Page title length | Page title length | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.titleLength.what` | The title is the blue clickable headline in Google results and the text on the browser tab. The length is counted in characters, spaces included. | The title is the blue clickable headline in Google results and the text on the browser tab. The length is counted in characters, spaces included. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.titleLength.why` | Google shows about 50 to 60 characters (roughly 580 pixels). Longer titles are cut off with an ellipsis, so your key words and brand can vanish. Titles that are too short waste a chance to describe the page and attract clicks. | Google shows about 50 to 60 characters (roughly 580 pixels). Longer titles are cut off with an ellipsis, so your key words and brand can vanish. Titles that are too short waste a chance to describe the page and attract clicks. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.titleLength.action` | Aim for 40 to 65 characters (SEOmi shows green in this range). Put the main keyword near the start. Make every page title unique. Add your brand at the end if it fits, for example after a dash. Write for people: promise what the visitor will get. Save and re-audit to see the counter turn green. | Aim for 40 to 65 characters (SEOmi shows green in this range). Put the main keyword near the start. Make every page title unique. Add your brand at the end if it fits, for example after a dash. Write for people: promise what the visitor will get. Save and re-audit to see the counter turn green. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.titleLength.example` | "Cheap shoes" (11 characters) is too short. "Buy Men's Running Shoes Online \| Free Returns \| ShoeShop" is 53 characters and fits. | "Cheap shoes" (11 characters) is too short. "Buy Men's Running Shoes Online \| Free Returns \| ShoeShop" is 53 characters and fits. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.metaDescription.title` | Meta description | Meta description | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.metaDescription.what` | A one or two sentence summary of the page, hidden in the code, that Google often shows under the title in search results. It is not visible on the page itself. | A one or two sentence summary of the page, hidden in the code, that Google often shows under the title in search results. It is not visible on the page itself. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.metaDescription.why` | It does not directly change your ranking, but a clear, inviting description raises the share of people who click your result (click-through rate), which brings more visitors from the same position. When it is missing Google makes up its own snippet, often a random piece of text. | It does not directly change your ranking, but a clear, inviting description raises the share of people who click your result (click-through rate), which brings more visitors from the same position. When it is missing Google makes up its own snippet, often a random piece of text. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.metaDescription.action` | Write a unique description for each page. Keep it between 120 and 160 characters so it is not cut off. Include the main keyword naturally, because Google bolds words that match the search. Say what the visitor gains and add a call to action such as "See prices" or "Learn how". Do not copy the first paragraph of the page. | Write a unique description for each page. Keep it between 120 and 160 characters so it is not cut off. Include the main keyword naturally, because Google bolds words that match the search. Say what the visitor gains and add a call to action such as "See prices" or "Learn how". Do not copy the first paragraph of the page. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.metadata.metaDescription.example` | "Learn how to choose running shoes in 5 steps: fit, cushioning, weight, price and terrain. Includes a free size chart and buying tips." (about 135 characters). | "Learn how to choose running shoes in 5 steps: fit, cushioning, weight, price and terrain. Includes a free size chart and buying tips." (about 135 characters). | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.headings.h1Count.what` | H1 is the main heading of a page, the biggest title the visitor sees at the top of the content. It is different from the title tag in the browser tab. Headings H2 to H6 are smaller sub-headings below it. | H1 is the main heading of a page, the biggest title the visitor sees at the top of the content. It is different from the title tag in the browser tab. Headings H2 to H6 are smaller sub-headings below it. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.headings.h1Count.why` | The H1 tells visitors and search engines what the page is about in one line. Pages without an H1 look unstructured to screen readers. Several H1s are not an error for Google, but they dilute the message and confuse assistive tools. | The H1 tells visitors and search engines what the page is about in one line. Pages without an H1 look unstructured to screen readers. Several H1s are not an error for Google, but they dilute the message and confuse assistive tools. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.headings.h1Count.action` | Exactly one H1 per page is the target. If there are 0: add one at the top of the content that describes the page, with the main keyword. If there are 2 or more: keep the best one and change the others to H2 or H3. Do not use H1 only for styling; use CSS for size instead. | Exactly one H1 per page is the target. If there are 0: add one at the top of the content that describes the page, with the main keyword. If there are 2 or more: keep the best one and change the others to H2 or H3. Do not use H1 only for styling; use CSS for size instead. | same-as-en |  |

| [ ] | `help.images.overview.why` | Images are usually the heaviest part of a page, so they slow it down. Missing alt text hurts blind visitors and removes a chance to appear in Google Images. Missing width and height make the page jump while loading (layout shift). | Images are usually the heaviest part of a page, so they slow it down. Missing alt text hurts blind visitors and removes a chance to appear in Google Images. Missing width and height make the page jump while loading (layout shift). | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.images.overview.action` | Give every meaningful image a short, accurate alt text. Use an empty alt for decorative ones. Lazy-load images that are below the first screen. | Give every meaningful image a short, accurate alt text. Use an empty alt for decorative ones. | [ ] | `help.links.overview.what` | The count of all links SEOmi found, split into internal (to your own site), external (to other sites) and nofollow (links marked so that search engines do not pass trust through them). | The count of all links SEOmi found, split into internal (to your own site), external (to other sites) and nofollow (links marked so that search engines do not pass trust through them). | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.links.overview.why` | Internal links help Google discover pages and understand which ones matter. External links to good sources make content more trustworthy. Broken links frustrate visitors and waste crawl budget. | Internal links help Google discover pages and understand which ones matter. External links to good sources make content more trustworthy. Broken links frustrate visitors and waste crawl budget. | same-as-en |  |
| [ ] | `help.links.overview.action` | Check the page links to your important related pages with descriptive text, not "click here". Fix or remove any link that returns 404. Review security risks, for example links opening a new tab without rel="noopener". | Check the page links to your important related pages with descriptive text, not "click here".
